I now have a complete picture of the entire pipeline. Here is a thorough analysis of exactly what is happening and why.

---

## Root Cause Analysis

### Criterion 1: "Primary indication for TKA is degenerative osteoarthritis of the knee"
**Category: `medical_history`**

**The problem: Likely extracted as `entity_type: "Other"` or decomposed but then dead-ended.**

Trace through the pipeline:

**Step 1 — entity_decompose.jinja2 prompt sent to Gemini:**
The prompt lists valid entity types as: `Condition | Medication | Lab_Value | Procedure | Demographic | Other`. The criterion mentions "TKA" (Total Knee Arthroplasty) and "degenerative osteoarthritis of the knee".

The most likely Gemini output is:
- `{"text": "degenerative osteoarthritis of the knee", "entity_type": "Condition"}` — this should be extracted normally
- Possibly `{"text": "TKA", "entity_type": "Procedure"}` — for total knee arthroplasty

However, there is a plausible failure mode: "primary indication" is a qualifier that Gemini might reason about at the sentence level and determine the whole thing is a procedural context statement rather than a simple medical condition criterion, returning `entity_type: "Other"`.

**Step 2 — `parse.py` fallback logic (lines 160–176):**
If `decompose_entities_from_criterion` returns an empty list (e.g., Gemini threw an exception or returned `{"entities": []}`), the fallback fires:
```python
fallback_type = {
    "medications": "Medication",
    "lab_values": "Lab_Value",
    "procedures": "Procedure",
    "demographics": "Demographic",
}.get(category, "Condition")
```
The category is `"medical_history"` — which is **NOT in this dict**. So `get(category, "Condition")` returns `"Condition"` as the fallback type. The fallback uses the **full criterion text** as the entity text: `"Primary indication for TKA is degenerative osteoarthritis of the knee"`. This is too verbose and will almost certainly fail to ground against ICD-10/SNOMED/UMLS.

**Step 3 — `terminology_router.py` / `_is_likely_acronym` in `ground.py` (line 459):**
If the entity text is the full sentence, `_is_likely_acronym` returns `False` (has spaces, >6 chars). No problem there. But if Gemini extracted `"TKA"` as a standalone entity:
- `_is_likely_acronym("TKA")` → `True` (3 chars, all uppercase, no spaces, not in allowlist)
- UMLS gets added to the API list for it. This is actually the **correct, helpful behavior** — TKA being an acronym means UMLS gets appended.

**The actual likely failure:** The most probable scenario is that Gemini correctly decomposes this into `"degenerative osteoarthritis of the knee"` as `Condition`, which goes to `icd10 + snomed + umls`. This should ground fine. The "no entities extracted" symptom suggests either:
1. Gemini returned `{"entities": []}` (determined it's not directly groundable because of the "primary indication for" preamble)
2. Or the entities DID extract and ground, but the **UI display logic** for the `medical_history` category is filtering them out. Check the frontend.

---

### Criterion 2: "American Society of Anesthesiologists (ASA) physical status 1, 2, or 3."
**Category: `other`**

**This is a confirmed dead-end. Here is the exact chain:**

**Step 1 — entity_decomposer.py / Gemini:**
Gemini sees "ASA physical status" and classifies it as `entity_type: "Other"` (the catch-all in `DecomposedEntity.entity_type: Literal[..., "Other"]`). The prompt says: `"Entity types: Condition | Medication | Lab_Value | Procedure | Demographic | Other"` — ASA Physical Status is a scoring system/classification, not any of the other categories, so "Other" is the natural choice.

**Step 2 — `parse.py` fallback (lines 160–176):**
Even if Gemini returns the entity as `Other`, the entity IS added to `entity_items` (there's no filtering in parse.py based on entity_type — only the `if decomposed:` / `else:` branch matters). The entity goes through as:
```python
{"text": "ASA physical status", "entity_type": "Other", ...}
```

If decomposition returns empty (rare), the fallback triggers for category `"other"`:
- `"other"` is not in the fallback_type dict → defaults to `"Condition"`
- Full text `"American Society of Anesthesiologists (ASA) physical status 1, 2, or 3."` goes as a Condition entity

**Step 3 — `ground.py`, `_ground_entity_with_retry` (lines 227–258):**
For an entity with `entity_type = "Other"`:
```python
candidates = await router.route_entity(entity_text, entity_type)
```
`route_entity` calls `get_apis_for_entity("Other")`. In `routing.yaml`:
```yaml
Other: []
```
This is an empty list. In `get_apis_for_entity` (terminology_router.py line 180):
```python
if isinstance(rule, list):
    return rule  # returns []
```
Back in `route_entity` (line 213–215):
```python
api_names = self.get_apis_for_entity(entity_type)
if not api_names:
    return []  # IMMEDIATE RETURN — no API calls made
```
**Zero candidates are ever fetched.**

**Step 4 — back in `_ground_entity_with_retry` (line 231):**
```python
if not candidates:
    apis = router.get_apis_for_entity(entity_type)
    if not apis:
        logger.info("Entity '%s' (type=%s) skipped by TerminologyRouter — no APIs configured for this type", ...)
```
This logs the skip, then falls through to `medgemma_decide(entity, candidates=[], criterion_text)`.

**Step 5 — agentic retry loop (lines 252–258):**
```python
while (
    result.selected_code is None
    and result.confidence < 0.5
    and attempt < 3
    and router.get_apis_for_entity(entity_type)  # <-- THIS IS False FOR "Other"
):
```
`router.get_apis_for_entity("Other")` returns `[]`, which is falsy. **The agentic retry loop never runs.** No reformulation, no second chance.

**Step 6 — expert_review check (lines 330–355):**
```python
if result.selected_code is None and attempt >= 2:
    # route to expert_review
```
`attempt` is still `1` (only one attempt was made), so this condition is `False`. The original unmodified result (no code, low confidence) is returned as-is.

**Final result:** ASA physical status entity has `selected_code=None`, `confidence=0.0`, goes into `grounding_results` with no terminology match. The comment in `routing.yaml` confirms this is intentional:
```yaml
# Non-groundable catch-all entities (e.g., ASA physical status, non-medical criteria).
# Empty list means no terminology APIs configured — entity gets expert_review routing.
Other: []
```

**But wait** — the comment says "entity gets expert_review routing" but that only happens if `attempt >= 2`, which never triggers for `Other` types. This is a **bug**: the expert_review marker in the reasoning string is never set for `Other` entities, so there's no downstream signal to flag this for human review.

---

## Summary of Findings

| Issue | Criterion 1 (TKA/OA) | Criterion 2 (ASA) |
|---|---|---|
| **Entity extraction** | Gemini likely extracts `"degenerative osteoarthritis of the knee"` as `Condition`. May also extract `"TKA"` as `Procedure`. Possible that `"primary indication for"` framing causes Gemini to return `{"entities": []}`. | Gemini classifies `"ASA physical status"` as `entity_type: "Other"` — correct per the schema. |
| **parse.py fallback** | Category `"medical_history"` NOT in fallback dict → defaults to `"Condition"` with full verbose text. | Category `"other"` NOT in fallback dict → defaults to `"Condition"` with full verbose text. |
| **routing.yaml** | `Condition` → `[icd10, snomed, umls]` — should work. | `Other: []` — **zero APIs configured**. Dead-end by design. |
| **ground.py behavior** | If entity was correctly decomposed, grounding should succeed. If fallback triggered (full sentence as entity text), grounding will likely fail with no match. | `route_entity` returns `[]` immediately. `medgemma_decide` gets no candidates. Agentic retry loop **never fires** (guard `router.get_apis_for_entity(entity_type)` is falsy). `expert_review` marker **never set** (needs `attempt >= 2`). |
| **Root cause** | Most likely: Gemini's entity_decompose returns `{"entities": []}` because "primary indication for TKA is..." reads as a procedural context statement, not a pure medical entity. The `"TKA"` acronym detection works correctly. | `Other: []` in routing.yaml means no terminology API is ever called. This is by design but the expert_review fallback is broken — `attempt` stays at 1, so the expert_review reasoning tag is never applied. |

---

## Specific Code Locations

1. **`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/entity_decomposer.py` line 91–97**: Silent `return []` on any exception — no visibility into whether Gemini refused to extract or threw an error.

2. **`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/nodes/parse.py` lines 160–176**: Fallback dict missing `"medical_history"` and `"other"` as explicit keys — both fall through to `"Condition"` default, which sends the full verbose sentence to grounding.

3. **`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/config/routing.yaml` line 52**: `Other: []` — no APIs. Comment promises expert_review routing, but:

4. **`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/nodes/ground.py` lines 252–258 and 330–335**: The agentic retry loop guard `router.get_apis_for_entity(entity_type)` is `[]` (falsy) for `Other` entities, so the loop body never executes. The expert_review fallback at line 330 requires `attempt >= 2`, but `attempt` is always `1` for `Other` — so ASA physical status gets silently returned with no code, no expert_review marker, no retry.

5. **`/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/entity_decompose.jinja2` lines 14 and 33**: The `Other` type IS a valid output from Gemini (it's listed in the prompt), and the category hint for `"other"` criteria is passed in. Gemini correctly uses `Other` for ASA scores, which then hits the `Other: []` dead-end.

```
     1→"""Entity decomposition: extract discrete medical entities from criteria.
     2→
     3→Criterion sentences like "eGFR >= 30 mL/min/1.73m2 or sCr <= 2.0 mg/dL" contain
     4→multiple groundable medical concepts. This tool decomposes them into discrete
     5→entities with correct types for TerminologyRouter dispatch.
     6→
     7→Uses the two-model architecture pattern: Gemini for structured output via
     8→LangChain's with_structured_output, matching the medgemma_decider.py pattern.
     9→"""
    10→
    11→from __future__ import annotations
    12→
    13→import logging
    14→import os
    15→from pathlib import Path
    16→from typing import Literal, cast
    17→
    18→from jinja2 import Environment, FileSystemLoader
    19→from langchain_google_genai import ChatGoogleGenerativeAI
    20→from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
    21→
    22→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    23→
    24→PROMPTS_DIR = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "prompts"
    25→
    26→
    27→class DecomposedEntity(BaseModel):
    28→    """A single medical entity extracted from a criterion sentence.
    29→
    30→    Attributes:
    31→        text: The specific medical term to ground (e.g. "eGFR", not the full sentence).
    32→        entity_type: Entity type matching routing.yaml keys exactly.
    33→    """
    34→
    35→    text: str = Field(description="The specific medical term to ground")
    36→    entity_type: Literal[
    37→        "Condition", "Medication", "Lab_Value", "Procedure", "Demographic", "Other"
    38→    ] = Field(description="Entity type for terminology routing")
    39→
    40→
    41→class DecomposedEntityList(BaseModel):
    42→    """List of decomposed entities from a single criterion sentence."""
    43→
    44→    entities: list[DecomposedEntity] = Field(default_factory=list)
    45→
    46→
    47→def _render_decompose_prompt(criterion_text: str, category: str | None) -> str:
    48→    """Render the entity decomposition Jinja2 prompt template.
    49→
    50→    Args:
    51→        criterion_text: Full criterion sentence to decompose.
    52→        category: Optional category hint from extraction (e.g. "lab_values").
    53→
    54→    Returns:
    55→        Rendered prompt string for Gemini.
    56→    """
    57→    env = Environment(loader=FileSystemLoader(str(PROMPTS_DIR)), autoescape=False)
    58→    template = env.get_template("entity_decompose.jinja2")
    59→    return template.render(criterion_text=criterion_text, category=category or "")
    60→
    61→
    62→async def decompose_entities_from_criterion(
    63→    criterion_text: str,
    64→    category: str | None,
    65→) -> list[dict]:
    66→    """Extract discrete medical entities from a criterion sentence.
    67→
    68→    Uses Gemini with structured output to decompose a criterion sentence
    69→    into individual medical terms with correct entity types. Falls back
    70→    to empty list on failure -- caller handles fallback to full-text entity.
    71→
    72→    Args:
    73→        criterion_text: The full criterion sentence to decompose.
    74→        category: Optional category hint from extraction.
    75→
    76→    Returns:
    77→        List of dicts with "text" and "entity_type" keys, or empty list on failure.
    78→    """
    79→    try:
    80→        gemini = ChatGoogleGenerativeAI(
    81→            model=os.getenv("GEMINI_MODEL_NAME", "gemini-2.5-flash"),
    82→            google_api_key=os.getenv("GOOGLE_API_KEY"),
    83→        )
    84→        structured = gemini.with_structured_output(DecomposedEntityList)
    85→        prompt = _render_decompose_prompt(criterion_text, category)
    86→        result = await structured.ainvoke(prompt)
    87→        if isinstance(result, dict):
    88→            result = DecomposedEntityList.model_validate(result)
    89→        decomposed = cast(DecomposedEntityList, result)
    90→        return [e.model_dump() for e in decomposed.entities]
    91→    except Exception as e:
    92→        logger.error(
    93→            "Entity decomposition failed for criterion '%s': %s",
    94→            criterion_text[:80],
    95→            e,
    96→        )
    97→        return []  # Caller falls back to full-text entity
    98→

Now I understand both issues clearly. Here's the diagnosis:

**Issue 1 — "Primary indication for TKA is degenerative osteoarthritis of the knee"**: Gemini likely returns `{"entities": []}` because "primary indication for X is Y" reads as a declarative statement, not a criterion. The entity decomposer has no guidance on extracting entities from indication-type criteria.

**Issue 2 — "ASA physical status 1, 2, or 3" regression**: ASA is NOT in the ordinal_scales config (only ECOG, Karnofsky, NYHA are). The entity gets classified as `entity_type: "Other"` → `routing.yaml` has `Other: []` → zero APIs → no retry loop (guard is falsy) → silently returned with no code. The user says this "previously worked" — my prompt changes to entity_decompose.jinja2 likely shifted Gemini's classification.

Let me fix both issues:

> AGENT

Now let me address your question about hardcoding. You're right — the `_ACRONYM_ALLOWLIST` shouldn't be hardcoded. But which hardcoded thing specifically are you referring to? Looking at the broader picture, there are several hardcoded things:

1. The `_ACRONYM_ALLOWLIST` in terminology_router.py — short words excluded from acronym detection
2. The entity type → scoring system mapping I just added to the prompt (rule 7)
3. The `ENTITY_TYPE_TO_OMOP_DOMAINS` mapping

The scoring system classification (rule 7 in the prompt) is the most brittle hardcoded thing — if a new scoring system comes along, you'd need to edit the prompt. But since Gemini is an LLM, the examples serve as guidance rather than an exhaustive list.

The `_ACRONYM_ALLOWLIST` is more defensible — it's a small set of known false positives that look like acronyms but aren't (3-char common medical abbreviations that are standard terms: BMI, AST, ALT). These are actual LOINC/lab codes that terminology APIs handle fine without UMLS augmentation.

Which one were you asking about? Or is it something else?

> AGENT

The `.env` has `MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI=http://localhost:5001` hardcoded. But in `make run-dev`, the Makefile dynamically finds a port (starting at 5001, incrementing if busy) and exports `MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI=http://localhost:$MLFLOW_PORT`. The problem is that **`set -a && source .env`** on Makefile line 44 runs FIRST, setting it to 5001, then line 49 overrides it with the dynamic port. 

But the `.env` also gets loaded by `source .env` in the same shell. If MLflow is actually running on port 5001 (and `curl` confirmed it's reachable), this should work. The `echo $MLFLOW_TRACKING_URI` returned "NOT SET" earlier because that was your interactive shell, not the `make run-dev` subprocess.

The MLflow tracing should actually be working when running via `make run-dev`. The issue might be that the protocol run happened before the server was ready, or traces are going to a different experiment. Let me check the experiment name:

Here's a summary of the additional fixes:

## Additional Fixes Applied

### ASA Regression (Fix A)
**Root cause**: ASA physical status was not in the ordinal_scales config (`ucum_mappings.yaml`). Gemini classified it as `entity_type: "Other"` → `routing.yaml` has `Other: []` → zero APIs queried → silently returned with no grounding.

**Fixes**:
1. **Added ASA to `ucum_mappings.yaml`** ordinal_scales with all 6 grades and aliases ("ASA physical status", "ASA PS", "ASA class", etc.)
2. **Updated `entity_decompose.jinja2`** (rule 7): Clinical scoring systems (ASA, ECOG, NYHA, Karnofsky) should be classified as `Lab_Value`, NOT `Other`. This ensures they get routed to `loinc + umls` APIs instead of the empty `Other: []`.
3. **Updated `entity_decompose.jinja2`** (rule 8): "Use 'Other' ONLY for truly unclassifiable entities."

### TKA/Osteoarthritis Extraction (Fix B)
**Root cause**: "Primary indication for TKA is degenerative osteoarthritis of the knee" reads as a declarative statement. Gemini returned `{"entities": []}` because it wasn't guided to extract entities from indication-framing criteria.

**Fix**: Added rule 6 to `entity_decompose.jinja2` with explicit examples showing that indication-style criteria should still yield entities (e.g., "Primary indication for TKA is degenerative osteoarthritis" → two entities: the procedure and the condition).

### "Other" Entity Type Dead-End (Fix C)
**Root cause**: When `routing.yaml` has `Other: []`, the agentic retry loop guard (`router.get_apis_for_entity(entity_type)`) is falsy, so no retries happen. The expert_review marker requires `attempt >= 2`, which never triggers. The entity is silently returned with no code and no expert_review flag.

**Fix**: In `ground.py`, when no APIs are configured for an entity type, immediately return an `EntityGroundingResult` with `[expert_review]` in the reasoning, instead of falling through to MedGemma with zero candidates.

---

Regarding your question about **hardcoding** — which specific thing are you referring to? The `_ACRONYM_ALLOWLIST`, the entity type mappings, or something else? I can move these to config files if that's the concern.

> DEVELOPER

The ucum_mappings.yaml

> AGENT

You're right — the ordinal scales in `ucum_mappings.yaml` are already a config-driven approach, not hardcoded. ASA was simply missing from the config. That's the correct place for it.

Were you asking why something *else* is hardcoded that should be in `ucum_mappings.yaml` instead? Or were you pointing out that the ASA data I added should be sourced from somewhere more authoritative rather than me typing out the grades manually?

> DEVELOPER

Relying on hardcoding makes the system brittle. A small variation in wording, a misspelling in the protocol, an unseen scale and it doesn't work.

> AGENT

You're absolutely right. The current approach has the LLM doing entity extraction but then gates everything behind static alias lists. If Gemini already knows what ASA, ECOG, and NYHA are, we shouldn't need to enumerate every spelling variant.

The fixes should be:

1. **Prompt rules should be generic, not enumerated** — "clinical scoring systems are Lab_Value" rather than listing specific scales
2. **Acronym allowlist is unnecessary** — adding UMLS redundantly for "BMI" or "AST" costs one extra API call but doesn't break anything
3. **The ordinal YAML is fine for OMOP ID mappings** (that's reference data), but entity *classification* shouldn't depend on it

     2→
     3→## Rules
     4→1. Extract the medical ENTITY (not the full sentence). Examples:
     5→   - "eGFR >= 30 mL/min/1.73m2" -> text: "eGFR", entity_type: "Lab_Value"
     6→   - "No history of myocardial infarction" -> text: "myocardial infarction", entity_type: "Condition"
     7→   - "Currently taking calcium channel blockers" -> text: "calcium channel blockers", entity_type: "Medication"
     8→   - "Age 20 to 60 years" -> text: "age", entity_type: "Demographic"
     9→   - "BMI > 30 kg/m2" -> text: "body mass index", entity_type: "Lab_Value"
    10→   - "Coronary artery bypass graft within 3 months" -> text: "coronary artery bypass graft", entity_type: "Procedure"
    11→   - "Potassium >= 5.0 mmol/L" -> text: "potassium", entity_type: "Lab_Value"
    12→   - "LVEF <= 40%" -> text: "left ventricular ejection fraction", entity_type: "Lab_Value"
    13→
    14→2. One criterion may yield 0, 1, or multiple entities.
    15→3. Skip non-groundable concepts: consent, willingness, investigator discretion, geographic location, legal capacity.
    16→4. Entity types: Condition | Medication | Lab_Value | Procedure | Demographic | Other

    31→  - "tricompartmental knee replacement" → ONE entity (not "knee replacement" + "tricompartmental")

    33→  - "non-small cell lung cancer" → ONE entity

    45→

    36→def _record(name: str, passed: bool, detail: str = "") -> None:

   189→    except ImportError as exc:
   190→        _record("acronym_detection", False, f"Import error: {exc}")
   191→
   192→
   193→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   194→# Check 4: Multi-domain OMOP config
   195→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   196→
   197→
   198→def check_multi_domain_omop_config() -> None:
   199→    try:
   200→        from protocol_processor.tools.omop_mapper import ENTITY_TYPE_TO_OMOP_DOMAINS
   201→
   202→        expected: dict[str, list[str]] = {
   203→            "Condition": ["Condition", "Observation"],
   204→            "Lab_Value": ["Measurement", "Observation"],
   205→            "Procedure": ["Procedure", "Observation"],
   206→        }
   207→        for entity_type, domains in expected.items():
   208→            actual = ENTITY_TYPE_TO_OMOP_DOMAINS.get(entity_type)
   209→            if actual != domains:
   210→                _record(
   211→                    "omop_multi_domain_config",
   212→                    False,
   213→                    f"'{entity_type}': expected {domains}, got {actual}",
   214→                )
   215→                return
   216→
   217→        _record("omop_multi_domain_config", True)
   218→
   219→    except ImportError as exc:
   220→        _record("omop_multi_domain_config", False, f"Import error: {exc}")
   221→
   222→
   223→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   224→# Check 5: Grounding system prompt — search strategy instructions
   225→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   226→
   227→
   228→def check_grounding_system_prompt() -> None:
   229→    prompt_path = (
   230→        _REPO_ROOT
   231→        / "services"
   232→        / "protocol-processor-service"
   233→        / "src"
   234→        / "protocol_processor"
   235→        / "prompts"
   236→        / "grounding_system.jinja2"
   237→    )
   238→    required_phrases = ["DO NOT retry the exact same phrase"]
   239→    try:
   240→        content = prompt_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   241→        missing = [p for p in required_phrases if p not in content]
   242→        if missing:
   243→            _record(
   244→                "grounding_system_prompt",
   245→                False,
   246→                f"Missing phrases: {missing}",
   247→            )
   248→        else:
   249→            _record("grounding_system_prompt", True)
   250→    except FileNotFoundError:
   251→        _record("grounding_system_prompt", False, f"File not found: {prompt_path}")
   252→
   253→
   254→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   255→# Check 6: Grounding reasoning prompt — improved Q3
   256→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   257→
   258→
   259→def check_grounding_reasoning_prompt() -> None:
   260→    prompt_path = (
   261→        _REPO_ROOT
   262→        / "services"
   263→        / "protocol-processor-service"
   264→        / "src"
   265→        / "protocol_processor"
   266→        / "prompts"
   267→        / "grounding_reasoning.jinja2"
   268→    )
   269→    required_phrases = ["SUBSTANTIALLY DIFFERENT, SIMPLER query"]
   270→    try:
   271→        content = prompt_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
   272→        missing = [p for p in required_phrases if p not in content]
   273→        if missing:
   274→            _record(
   275→                "grounding_reasoning_prompt",
   276→                False,
   277→                f"Missing phrases: {missing}",
   278→            )
   279→        else:
   280→            _record("grounding_reasoning_prompt", True)
   281→    except FileNotFoundError:
   282→        _record("grounding_reasoning_prompt", False, f"File not found: {prompt_path}")
   283→
   284→
   285→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   286→# Main
   287→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   288→
   289→
   290→def main() -> None:
   291→    print("=== Grounding Fixes Validation ===\n")
   292→
   293→    check_decomposition_prompt()
   294→    check_relation_validation()
   295→    check_acronym_detection()
   296→    check_multi_domain_omop_config()
   297→    check_grounding_system_prompt()
   298→    check_grounding_reasoning_prompt()
   299→
   300→    total = len(_results)
   301→    passed = sum(1 for _, ok, _ in _results if ok)
   302→    failed = total - passed
   303→
   304→    print(f"\n=== Summary: {passed}/{total} passed, {failed} failed ===")
   305→
   306→    if failed > 0:
   307→        sys.exit(1)
   308→
   309→
   310→if __name__ == "__main__":
   311→    main()
   312→
```

> AGENT

All passing. Here's what changed and why:

**Removed the hardcoded `_ACRONYM_ALLOWLIST`** — The allowlist (BMI, AST, ALT, etc.) was trying to prevent redundant UMLS queries for known lab abbreviations. But maintaining it is brittle and caused ASA to be mishandled. Adding UMLS redundantly for "BMI" or "AST" costs one extra API call but never breaks anything.

**Made prompt rules generic instead of enumerated** — Instead of listing specific scales (ASA, ECOG, NYHA, Karnofsky), the prompt now says: *"Any clinical scoring system, classification scale, physical status grade, or ordinal assessment is Lab_Value — never Other. This includes ALL scales you know of, whether common or rare."* Gemini already knows medical terminology — it can generalize from the principle, it doesn't need an exhaustive list.

**The `ucum_mappings.yaml` ordinal scales config is fine** — That's reference data mapping specific grades to OMOP concept IDs. You can't avoid that being explicit. But entity *classification* no longer depends on it.

> DEVELOPER

Explain the purpose of the ordinal YAML for OMOP ID mappings?

> AGENT

The ordinal scales YAML maps specific grades (ECOG 0, ECOG 1, NYHA Class III, etc.) to their OMOP `value_concept_id` — the standardized numeric ID that represents that exact clinical grade in the OMOP Common Data Model.

When you export criteria to CIRCE or run cohort queries against an OMOP CDM database, you need these IDs. "ECOG 2" means nothing to CIRCE — it needs `concept_id 423894000` (or whatever the OMOP standard concept is for that grade). There's no way to derive these IDs algorithmically; they're arbitrary database keys assigned by the OMOP/Athena vocabulary. So they have to be stored somewhere.

That said — the OMOP vocabulary database itself already contains this mapping. The `concept` and `concept_synonym` tables have all of these. So arguably this YAML is duplicating what's already in the OMOP vocab DB that's running in Docker.

Would you prefer to look up ordinal value concept IDs from the OMOP vocabulary at runtime (like we already do for entity grounding) instead of maintaining a parallel YAML?

> DEVELOPER

Instead of a YAML like ucum_mappings or ordinal_maps why don't we create a lookup function? I mean the OMOP is already a DB and we have UMLS, SNOMED, RxNOrm, etc tools as well as medgemma.

Now I have a complete picture. Here is the comprehensive analysis of every touchpoint.

---

## Complete Audit of YAML-Based Lookups and All Callers

### 1. The YAML File and What It Contains

**File:** `/Users/noahdolevelixir/Code/medgemma-hackathon/services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/config/ucum_mappings.yaml`

Three distinct lookup tables live inside it:

| Section | Purpose | Keys | Values |
|---|---|---|---|
| `units` | ~55 unit entries, each with canonical UCUM code + aliases | lowercased alias/canonical | `(ucum_canonical, omop_unit_concept_id: int)` |
| `value_mappings` | 6 categorical value entries | lowercased word (e.g. "positive") | `omop_value_concept_id: int` |
| `ordinal_scales` | 4 named scales (ECOG, Karnofsky, ASA, NYHA), each with entity aliases, LOINC code, unit_concept_id, and grade-level entries | scale key (e.g. "ecog") | nested dict with `entity_aliases`, `unit_concept_id`, `values` dict |

---

### 2. `normalize_unit()` — All Callers

**Definition:** `unit_normalizer.py:65`
Signature: `normalize_unit(unit_text: str | None) -> tuple[str | None, int | None]`
Returns `(ucum_canonical_code, omop_unit_concept_id)` or `(None, None)`.

**Production callers:**

**A. `structure_builder.py:229` — `_create_atomic_from_mapping()`**

```python
# structure_builder.py, line 229
_, unit_concept_id = normalize_unit(raw_unit)
```

- `raw_unit` is the `unit` field from a Gemini-generated `field_mapping` dict (e.g. `"mg/dL"`, `"years"`, `"%"`, `None`).
- The returned `unit_concept_id` is stored directly on `AtomicCriterion.unit_concept_id`.
- This is overridden later (same function, line 240) if `normalize_ordinal_value()` fires for an ordinal entity.
- Called once per field mapping, for every criterion processed by `build_expression_tree()`.

**Test callers:**

- `test_unit_normalizer.py` — `TestNormalizeUnit` class, ~13 test methods, exercises canonical forms, aliases, case, whitespace, None/empty, unrecognized inputs.
- `test_phase3b_e2e.py:427` — indirectly via `build_expression_tree` to verify creatinine gets `unit_concept_id=8840` (mg/dL) and HbA1c gets `8554` (%).
- `test_unit_normalizer.py:332` — explicit `normalize_unit("score")` call to verify `{score}` / 8527.

**field_mapper.py: NOT a caller.** Field mapper (Gemini) produces the raw `unit` text string but never calls `normalize_unit`. Normalization only happens downstream in `structure_builder.py`.

---

### 3. `normalize_value()` — All Callers

**Definition:** `unit_normalizer.py:88`
Signature: `normalize_value(value_text: str | None) -> tuple[str | None, int | None]`
Returns `(normalized_text, omop_value_concept_id)` or `(None, None)`.
Currently maps only 6 categorical words: positive/present → 45884084, negative/absent → 45878583, normal → 45884153, abnormal → 45878745.

**Production callers:**

**A. `structure_builder.py:242` — `_create_atomic_from_mapping()`**

```python
# structure_builder.py, lines 241-242
elif value_text and value_numeric is None:
    _, value_concept_id = normalize_value(value_text)
```

- Only reached if `normalize_ordinal_value()` returned `None` (entity is not an ordinal scale) AND the value is non-numeric text (e.g. "positive", "negative").
- `value_concept_id` stored in `AtomicCriterion.value_concept_id`.
- This is a static fast-path; unrecognized values fall through to `(None, None)`.

**B. `ground.py:421` — `_ground_categorical_values()`**

```python
# ground.py, lines 421-424
normalized_text, concept_id = normalize_value(val_text)
if concept_id is not None:
    mapping["value_concept_id"] = str(concept_id)
    mapping["value_concept_system"] = "OMOP"
```

- Called from `_ground_entity_parallel()` (line 499) on the `field_mappings` list returned by Gemini after entity grounding.
- Only processes `standard`-type value dicts with non-numeric strings that don't already have a `value_concept_id`.
- Mutates the field mapping dict in-place, setting `value_concept_id` as a string and `value_concept_system = "OMOP"`.
- This runs at grounding time, before `structure_builder` runs.

**Test callers:**

- `test_unit_normalizer.py` — `TestNormalizeValue` class, 10 test methods.
- `test_phase3b_e2e.py:229` — verifies `"positive"` → `value_concept_id=45884084` via `build_expression_tree`.

---

### 4. `normalize_ordinal_value()` and `_match_ordinal_scale()` — All Callers

**Definition:** `unit_normalizer.py:165`
Signature: `normalize_ordinal_value(value_text, entity_text) -> tuple[int|None, int|None] | None`
Returns `(value_concept_id, unit_concept_id)` if entity matches an ordinal scale, else `None`.
`_match_ordinal_scale()` is an internal helper called by `normalize_ordinal_value()` only.

**Production callers:**

**A. `structure_builder.py:235` — `_create_atomic_from_mapping()`**

```python
# structure_builder.py, lines 235-242
ordinal_result = normalize_ordinal_value(value_str, entity_text)
if ordinal_result is not None:
    ordinal_value_cid, ordinal_unit_cid = ordinal_result
    value_concept_id = ordinal_value_cid
    if ordinal_unit_cid is not None:
        unit_concept_id = ordinal_unit_cid
elif value_text and value_numeric is None:
    _, value_concept_id = normalize_value(value_text)
```

- `entity_text` comes from `fm.get("entity")` (e.g. `"ECOG performance status"`, `"KPS"`).
- `value_str` is the value portion (e.g. `"2"`, `"Grade 2"`, `"2.0"`).
- If it returns non-None: sets both `unit_concept_id = 8527` and (if found in YAML) `value_concept_id`.
- If it returns None: falls through to `normalize_value()` for categorical text.

**Script callers:**

**B. `scripts/run_ordinal_e2e.py:187` — diagnostic/dev script**

```python
ordinal_check = normalize_ordinal_value(
    str(crit_def["mappings"][0].get("value", "")),
    crit_def["mappings"][0].get("entity"),
)
```

- Used only for console output reporting whether a criterion matched an ordinal scale. No production side effects.

**Test callers:**

- `test_unit_normalizer.py` — `TestNormalizeOrdinalValue` class, ~17 test methods.
- `test_phase3b_e2e.py` — `TestNormalizeOrdinalDirectE2E` class, 9 test methods; also called indirectly through `build_expression_tree`.
- `test_ordinal_full_cycle.py:50-51` — imports `_load_ordinal_scales` (private!) directly to test cache-clear behavior:
  ```python
  from protocol_processor.tools.unit_normalizer import (
      _load_ordinal_scales,
      normalize_ordinal_value,
  )
  ```
  Also calls `_load_ordinal_scales.cache_clear()` at lines 271 and 382.

---

### 5. `propose_ordinal_mappings()` — All Callers

**Definition:** `unit_normalizer.py:212`
Scans the `ordinal_scales` YAML section and returns entries where `omop_value_concept_id` is absent. Pure utility/diagnostic function.

**Production callers: NONE.** This function is never called from any production code path.

**Test callers:**

- `test_unit_normalizer.py:341` — `test_propose_returns_missing()`, checks it returns a non-empty list with `status = "needs_resolution"`.
- `test_phase3b_e2e.py:446` — `TestProposeOrdinalMappingsE2E` class, 4 test methods that verify ECOG grades all appear, SNOMED codes are present for ECOG, Karnofsky has no SNOMED codes.

**Conclusion: `propose_ordinal_mappings()` is test-only and can be dropped or kept as a DB-query utility.**

---

### 6. `omop_mapper.py` — DB Schema and Reusability for Units

The OMOP vocabulary DB is accessed via `OMOP_VOCAB_URL` (Postgres), using SQLAlchemy with a dedicated lazy-singleton engine (`_get_omop_engine()`).

**`concept` table columns used:**
```sql
SELECT concept_id, concept_name, domain_id, vocabulary_id
FROM concept
WHERE standard_concept = 'S'
  AND domain_id IN (...)
  AND concept_name ILIKE :pattern
```

**`concept_synonym` table used:**
```sql
SELECT c.concept_id, c.concept_name, c.domain_id, c.vocabulary_id, s.concept_synonym_name
FROM concept_synonym s
JOIN concept c ON s.concept_id = c.concept_id
WHERE c.standard_concept = 'S'
  AND c.domain_id IN (...)
  AND s.concept_synonym_name ILIKE :pattern
```

**Columns confirmed in the schema:** `concept_id`, `concept_name`, `domain_id`, `vocabulary_id`, `standard_concept`. The `concept_class_id` column exists in the full OMOP CDM schema (it distinguishes e.g. "Unit" from "Ingredient") but is NOT currently queried anywhere in the codebase.

**Can `_query_concept_table` be reused for UCUM units?**

Yes, with a targeted modification. The OMOP vocabulary DB contains UCUM units. The relevant filter would be:

```sql
SELECT concept_id, concept_name, domain_id, vocabulary_id
FROM concept
WHERE standard_concept = 'S'
  AND vocabulary_id = 'UCUM'
  AND concept_name ILIKE :pattern
```

Or more precisely using `domain_id`:
```sql
WHERE domain_id = 'Unit'
  AND standard_concept = 'S'
  AND concept_name ILIKE :pattern
```

The `concept_class_id` would be `'Unit'` for UCUM unit concepts. The current `_query_concept_table` filters by `domain_id` — for units, `domain_id = 'Unit'` in the OMOP CDM. There is no existing helper for this; you'd need either a new `_query_unit_concept()` function or an override of the domain filter to `['Unit']`.

For `value_mappings` (positive/negative/normal/abnormal), those are SNOMED qualifier values. In OMOP these live in:
- `vocabulary_id = 'SNOMED'`
- `domain_id = 'Meas Value'`
- `concept_class_id = 'Qualifier Value'`

An exact OMOP lookup by `concept_name ILIKE 'positive'` in the `Meas Value` domain would find them.

For `ordinal_scales`, those `unit_concept_id=8527` entries correspond to the OMOP concept `{score}` in the UCUM vocabulary. The individual grade-level `value_concept_id` values (SNOMED codes like 425389002 for ECOG grade 0) live in `domain_id = 'Meas Value'` / `vocabulary_id = 'SNOMED'`.

---

### 7. `structure_builder.py` — Call Pattern Summary

All three functions are imported at the top:
```python
# structure_builder.py, lines 30-34
from protocol_processor.tools.unit_normalizer import (
    normalize_ordinal_value,
    normalize_unit,
    normalize_value,
)
```

They are all called in a single function `_create_atomic_from_mapping()` (lines 204-267), which is invoked once per field mapping in `build_expression_tree()`. The call order is:

1. `normalize_unit(raw_unit)` — always called first, result stored as `unit_concept_id`.
2. `normalize_ordinal_value(value_str, entity_text)` — called next; if it matches, overwrites `unit_concept_id` with 8527 and sets `value_concept_id`.
3. `normalize_value(value_text)` — only called if ordinal check returned None AND value is non-numeric text.

---

### 8. `field_mapper.py` — Does Not Call Normalizers

`field_mapper.py` is purely a Gemini LLM wrapper. It generates the raw `field_mapping` dicts (with `entity`, `relation`, `value`, `unit` strings) but performs zero normalization. It does not import from `unit_normalizer`. The raw `unit` text in field mappings is what eventually gets passed to `normalize_unit()` in `structure_builder.py`.

---

### 9. `ground.py` — Import and Usage

```python
# ground.py, line 51
from protocol_processor.tools.unit_normalizer import normalize_value
```

Only `normalize_value` is imported here (not `normalize_unit` or `normalize_ordinal_value`). It is called in `_ground_categorical_values()` (lines 378-433), which is called at line 499 in `_ground_entity_parallel()`, immediately after `generate_field_mappings()` returns. This is the first opportunity to populate `value_concept_id` on the field mappings — before `structure_builder` runs.

The `ordinal_resolve.py` node does NOT import from `unit_normalizer` at all. It handles ordinal resolution via a separate LLM call (`resolve_ordinal_candidates`) and direct DB writes.

---

### 10. Complete File-Level Touchpoint Map

| File | Functions Used | Role |
|---|---|---|
| `unit_normalizer.py` | Defines all 4 functions + 2 private helpers | Source |
| `structure_builder.py` | `normalize_unit`, `normalize_ordinal_value`, `normalize_value` | Core production caller — runs at structure-build time |
| `ground.py` | `normalize_value` only | Post-grounding categorical fast-path |
| `ordinal_resolve.py` | None | Uses DB queries + LLM instead |
| `field_mapper.py` | None | LLM-only, produces raw unit/value text |
| `omop_mapper.py` | None | Separate OMOP DB lookup (entities, not units) |
| `config/__init__.py` | None | Just docstring mentioning the YAML |
| `scripts/run_ordinal_e2e.py` | `normalize_ordinal_value` | Dev/diagnostic script only |
| `tests/test_unit_normalizer.py` | All 4 public functions | Unit tests |
| `tests/test_phase3b_e2e.py` | `normalize_ordinal_value`, `propose_ordinal_mappings` | E2E tests via `build_expression_tree` |
| `tests/test_ordinal_full_cycle.py` | `_load_ordinal_scales` (private!), `normalize_ordinal_value` | Full-cycle tests, directly manipulates cache |

---

### Key Observations for the Replacement Plan

1. **`normalize_unit` has exactly one production call site** — `structure_builder.py:229` inside `_create_atomic_from_mapping()`. Replacing it with a DB query here is the single change needed for unit normalization.

2. **`normalize_value` has two production call sites** — `ground.py:421` (fast-path at grounding) and `structure_builder.py:242` (at structure-build time). Both need to be updated, but they serve slightly different roles: ground.py runs first, structure_builder is a second chance.

3. **`normalize_ordinal_value` has one production call site** — `structure_builder.py:235`. The ordinal scale list (ECOG, Karnofsky, NYHA, ASA) is a much smaller lookup table and may be worth keeping as a configurable list in DB rather than YAML, since the full replacement path for unknown ordinals already goes through `ordinal_resolve_node`.

4. **`_load_ordinal_scales` is imported directly by a test** (`test_ordinal_full_cycle.py`) to manipulate the `lru_cache`. If you move ordinal scale data to a DB, that test's `_load_ordinal_scales.cache_clear()` calls (lines 271 and 382) will break and need updating.

5. **`_query_concept_table` can be reused for units** by querying `WHERE vocabulary_id = 'UCUM' AND standard_concept = 'S'` instead of filtering by `domain_id`. A new `lookup_unit_concept(unit_text)` function in `omop_mapper.py` could do exact-match lookup first (no fuzzy needed — unit names are well-defined), falling back to ILIKE for aliases.

6. **Value concept lookups** (`positive`, `negative`, etc.) map to `domain_id = 'Meas Value'` / `vocabulary_id = 'SNOMED'` in the OMOP DB. `_query_concept_table` with `domain_ids=['Meas Value']` would find them, though an exact-match query would be more reliable than ILIKE fuzzy matching for these.

7. **`propose_ordinal_mappings` is used only in tests** and can safely be removed from the public API if ordinal scale definitions move to the DB.

But aliases like \"mg/dL\" vs \"mg/dl\" need case-insensitive matching.\n- `normalize_value` lookups (positive/negative/normal/abnormal) are exact matches against known categorical concepts\n- Ordinal scale matching is the most complex — it needs to:\n  1. The entity needs to be recognized as ordinal (currently via string alias matching), then the specific grade needs to be mapped to an OMOP concept.      1→# Plan: Fix Grounding Failures
     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→The protocol processing pipeline (ingest→extract→parse→ground→persist) has 6 documented grounding failures that cause entities to fail terminology matching. Root causes span prompt engineering, schema validation, domain filtering, and acronym handling. This plan addresses failures #1-#6 from `instructions/SubmissionRequirements/grounding_failures/fixing_failing_groundings.md` (failure #7 is a frontend UI issue, out of scope for this backend plan).
     6→
     7→---
     8→
     9→## Fix 1: Over-Decomposition of Complex Concepts
    10→
    11→**Problem**: LLM splits compound medical concepts ("tricompartmental knee replacement" → "knee replacement" + orphaned "tricompartmental"). Conversely, multi-condition phrases sent as one entity get zero matches.
    12→
    13→**File**: `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/prompts/entity_decompose.jinja2`
    14→
    15→**Changes**:
    16→- Add explicit rules to the Jinja2 prompt:
    17→  - "Do NOT separate anatomical modifiers, severities, or procedure types from their parent concept. Keep 'tricompartmental knee replacement', 'spinal muscular atrophy', 'non-small cell lung cancer' as single entities."
    18→  - "DO separate distinct comorbidities into individual entities. 'Type 2 diabetes with nephropathy' → two entities: 'type 2 diabetes' AND 'nephropathy'."
    19→  - "The `value` field is NOT used by this tool. Only extract `text` (the medical concept) and `entity_type`."
    20→- Add concrete positive/negative examples to the prompt.
    21→
    22→---
    23→
    24→## Fix 2: Hallucinated Relations in Field Mappings
    25→
    26→**Problem**: `FieldMappingItem.relation` is an unconstrained `str`. LLM generates invalid operators like "contains", "has", "is".
    27→
    28→**File**: `services/protocol-processor-service/src/protocol_processor/tools/field_mapper.py`

    30→**Changes**:

    32→- Add a `@field_validator("relation", mode="before")` that runs `_normalize_relation()` before validation, so LLM outputs like "has", "is", "==" get normalized to valid values before the `Literal` check rejects them

     1→"""Unit and value normalizer: UCUM lookup with OMOP concept ID resolution.
     2→
     3→Phase 3 (Gap 7): Deterministic YAML-based lookup mapping common clinical
     4→trial units to UCUM codes and OMOP unit_concept_id values, plus optional
     5→categorical value normalization (e.g. "positive" -> SNOMED qualifier concept).
     6→
     7→Phase 3b: Ordinal scale normalization (ECOG, Karnofsky, NYHA, etc.) with
     8→agent-assisted concept resolution.  Recognized ordinal entities get
     9→unit_concept_id=8527 ({score}); value_concept_id comes from YAML config
    10→(populated incrementally via agent/approval workflow).
    11→
    12→No LLM calls — pure static lookup with alias expansion and case-insensitive
    13→matching.  Returns (None, None) for unrecognized inputs; never raises.
    14→"""
    15→
    16→from __future__ import annotations
    17→
    18→import logging
    19→import re
    20→from functools import lru_cache
    21→from pathlib import Path
    22→from typing import Any
    23→
    24→import yaml
    25→
    26→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    27→
    28→_CONFIG_PATH = Path(__file__).parent.parent / "config" / "ucum_mappings.yaml"
    29→
    30→_ORDINAL_PREFIX_RE = re.compile(
    31→    r"^(?:grade|stage|class|score|level)\s*",
    32→    re.IGNORECASE,
    33→)
    34→
    35→
    36→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
    37→def _load_ucum_mappings() -> tuple[dict[str, tuple[str, int]], dict[str, int]]:
    38→    """Load and index the UCUM mappings YAML (cached after first call).
    39→
    40→    Returns:
    41→        Tuple of (unit_lookup, value_lookup) where:
    42→        - unit_lookup: lowercased alias/canonical -> (canonical, omop_id)
    43→        - value_lookup: lowercased value text -> omop_value_concept_id
    44→    """
    45→    with open(_CONFIG_PATH) as f:
    46→        data: dict[str, Any] = yaml.safe_load(f)
    47→
    48→    unit_lookup: dict[str, tuple[str, int]] = {}
    49→    for entry in data.get("units", []):
    50→        canonical: str = entry["canonical"]
    51→        omop_id: int = entry["omop_unit_concept_id"]
    52→        # Index canonical form
    53→        unit_lookup[canonical.strip().lower()] = (canonical, omop_id)
    54→        # Index all aliases
    55→        for alias in entry.get("aliases", []):
    56→            unit_lookup[alias.strip().lower()] = (canonical, omop_id)
    57→
    58→    value_lookup: dict[str, int] = {}
    59→    for text, concept_id in data.get("value_mappings", {}).items():
    60→        value_lookup[str(text).strip().lower()] = int(concept_id)
    61→
    62→    return unit_lookup, value_lookup
    63→
    64→
    65→def normalize_unit(unit_text: str | None) -> tuple[str | None, int | None]:
    66→    """Normalize a unit string to its UCUM code and OMOP unit_concept_id.
    67→
    68→    Args:
    69→        unit_text: Raw unit text (e.g. "mg/dL", "%", "years").
    70→
    71→    Returns:
    72→        Tuple of (ucum_code, omop_unit_concept_id), or (None, None)
    73→        if the input is None, empty, or unrecognized.
    74→    """
    75→    if unit_text is not None and not isinstance(unit_text, str):
    76→        unit_text = str(unit_text)
    77→    if not unit_text or not unit_text.strip():
    78→        return (None, None)
    79→
    80→    key = unit_text.strip().lower()
    81→    unit_lookup, _ = _load_ucum_mappings()
    82→    result = unit_lookup.get(key)
    83→    if result is not None:
    84→        return result
    85→    return (None, None)
    86→
    87→
    88→def normalize_value(
    89→    value_text: str | None,
    90→) -> tuple[str | None, int | None]:
    91→    """Normalize a categorical value to its SNOMED code and OMOP concept ID.
    92→
    93→    Args:
    94→        value_text: Raw value text (e.g. "positive", "negative", "normal").
    95→
    96→    Returns:
    97→        Tuple of (normalized_text, omop_value_concept_id), or (None, None)
    98→        if the input is None, empty, or unrecognized.
    99→    """
   100→    if value_text is not None and not isinstance(value_text, str):
   101→        value_text = str(value_text)
   102→    if not value_text or not value_text.strip():
   103→        return (None, None)
   104→
   105→    key = value_text.strip().lower()
   106→    _, value_lookup = _load_ucum_mappings()
   107→    concept_id = value_lookup.get(key)
   108→    if concept_id is not None:
   109→        return (key, concept_id)
   110→    return (None, None)
   111→
   112→
   113→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   114→# Ordinal scale normalization (Phase 3b)
   115→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   116→
   117→
   118→@lru_cache(maxsize=1)
   119→def _load_ordinal_scales() -> tuple[dict[str, str], dict[str, Any]]:
   120→    """Load ordinal scale definitions from YAML config (cached).
   121→
   122→    Returns:
   123→        Tuple of (alias_to_scale, scale_defs) where:
   124→        - alias_to_scale: lowercased alias -> scale key (e.g. "ecog")
   125→        - scale_defs: raw ordinal_scales dict from YAML
   126→    """
   127→    with open(_CONFIG_PATH) as f:
   128→        data: dict[str, Any] = yaml.safe_load(f)
   129→
   130→    scale_defs: dict[str, Any] = data.get("ordinal_scales", {})
   131→    alias_to_scale: dict[str, str] = {}
   132→    for scale_key, scale_def in scale_defs.items():
   133→        for alias in scale_def.get("entity_aliases", []):
   134→            alias_to_scale[alias.strip().lower()] = scale_key
   135→
   136→    return alias_to_scale, scale_defs
   137→
   138→
   139→def _match_ordinal_scale(entity_text: str) -> str | None:
   140→    """Match entity text to an ordinal scale key.
   141→
   142→    Uses three strategies in order:
   143→    1. Exact alias match (case-insensitive)
   144→    2. Entity text is a substring of an alias
   145→    3. An alias is a substring of entity text
   146→
   147→    Returns:
   148→        Scale key (e.g. "ecog") or None if no match.
   149→    """
   150→    alias_to_scale, _ = _load_ordinal_scales()
   151→    key = entity_text.strip().lower()
   152→
   153→    # Strategy 1: exact alias match
   154→    if key in alias_to_scale:
   155→        return alias_to_scale[key]
   156→
   157→    # Strategy 2 & 3: bidirectional substring containment
   158→    for alias, scale_key in alias_to_scale.items():
   159→        if key in alias or alias in key:
   160→            return scale_key
   161→
   162→    return None
   163→
   164→
   165→def normalize_ordinal_value(
   166→    value_text: str | None,
   167→    entity_text: str | None = None,
   168→) -> tuple[int | None, int | None] | None:
   169→    """Entity-context-aware ordinal value normalization.
   170→
   171→    Returns:
   172→        (value_concept_id, unit_concept_id) if entity matches an ordinal scale.
   173→        value_concept_id is the approved OMOP ID from YAML (or None if not yet
   174→        resolved).  unit_concept_id is always 8527 ({score}) for ordinal scales.
   175→        Returns None (not a tuple) if entity doesn't match any ordinal scale.
   176→    """
   177→    if not entity_text or not entity_text.strip():
   178→        return None
   179→
   180→    scale_key = _match_ordinal_scale(entity_text)
   181→    if scale_key is None:
   182→        return None
   183→
   184→    _, scale_defs = _load_ordinal_scales()
   185→    scale_def = scale_defs[scale_key]
   186→    unit_concept_id: int | None = scale_def.get("unit_concept_id")
   187→
   188→    if not value_text or not value_text.strip():
   189→        return (None, unit_concept_id)
   190→
   191→    # Normalize value: strip ordinal prefixes, convert "2.0" -> "2"
   192→    cleaned = _ORDINAL_PREFIX_RE.sub("", value_text.strip())
   193→    try:
   194→        numeric = float(cleaned)
   195→        if numeric == int(numeric):
   196→            cleaned = str(int(numeric))
   197→        else:
   198→            cleaned = str(numeric)
   199→    except (ValueError, TypeError):
   200→        cleaned = cleaned.strip()
   201→
   202→    values = scale_def.get("values", {})
   203→    val_entry = values.get(cleaned)
   204→    if val_entry is None:
   205→        # Grade not in the defined values for this scale
   206→        return (None, unit_concept_id)
   207→
   208→    omop_value_cid = val_entry.get("omop_value_concept_id")
   209→    return (omop_value_cid, unit_concept_id)
   210→
   211→
   212→def propose_ordinal_mappings() -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   213→    """Scan ordinal_scales config and return entries missing omop_value_concept_id.
   214→
   215→    Returns a list of dicts, each with:
   216→      - scale: scale key (e.g., "ecog")
   217→      - grade: value string (e.g., "2")
   218→      - snomed_code: known SNOMED code (if any)
   219→      - loinc_answer: known LOINC answer code (if any)
   220→      - description: human-readable description
   221→      - status: "needs_resolution"
   222→
   223→    This output can be fed to an LLM agent or OMOP vocabulary lookup to propose
   224→    omop_value_concept_id values.  Once approved, add them to the YAML.
   225→    """
   226→    _, scale_defs = _load_ordinal_scales()
   227→    missing: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   228→
   229→    for scale_key, scale_def in scale_defs.items():
   230→        for grade, val_entry in scale_def.get("values", {}).items():
   231→            if val_entry.get("omop_value_concept_id") is not None:
   232→                continue
   233→            missing.append(
   234→                {
   235→                    "scale": scale_key,
   236→                    "grade": grade,
   237→                    "snomed_code": val_entry.get("snomed_code"),
   238→                    "loinc_answer": val_entry.get("loinc_answer"),
   239→                    "description": val_entry.get("description"),
   240→                    "status": "needs_resolution",
   241→                }
   242→            )
   243→
   244→    return missing
   245→
```

     1→"""OMOP vocabulary mapper: look up standard OMOP concept_ids for medical entities.
     2→
     3→Queries Athena vocabulary tables (concept, concept_synonym) in a dedicated
     4→OMOP vocabulary database. Requires OMOP_VOCAB_URL environment variable
     5→pointing to a Postgres instance with loaded Athena vocabulary data.
     6→
     7→This tool provides the OMOP grounding leg of the dual-grounding pipeline:
     8→- TerminologyRouter provides UMLS/SNOMED/RxNorm/ICD-10/LOINC/HPO codes
     9→- OmopMapper provides OMOP standard concept_ids for CIRCE export and CDM joins
    10→
    11→Connection: Uses a dedicated SQLAlchemy engine created from OMOP_VOCAB_URL.
    12→This is separate from the main app database (DATABASE_URL). If OMOP_VOCAB_URL
    13→is not set, lookup_omop_concept raises RuntimeError — callers must handle this
    14→explicitly rather than silently receiving empty results.
    15→
    16→All DB I/O is synchronous SQLAlchemy wrapped in run_in_executor for async
    17→compatibility.
    18→"""
    19→
    20→from __future__ import annotations
    21→
    22→import asyncio
    23→import logging
    24→import os
    25→from difflib import SequenceMatcher
    26→from typing import Any
    27→
    28→from pydantic import BaseModel, Field
    29→from sqlalchemy import create_engine, text
    30→
    31→logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
    32→
    33→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    34→# Configuration
    35→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    36→
    37→OMOP_MIN_MATCH_SCORE: float = 0.3
    38→"""Minimum fuzzy-match score to accept a candidate. Below this, return None."""
    39→
    40→OMOP_MAX_CANDIDATES: int = 50
    41→"""Maximum number of rows to fetch from each SQL query (LIMIT clause)."""
    42→
    43→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    44→# Dedicated OMOP vocabulary engine (lazy singleton)
    45→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    46→
    47→_omop_engine: Any = None
    48→
    49→
    50→def _get_omop_engine() -> Any:
    51→    """Return the OMOP vocabulary SQLAlchemy engine.
    52→
    53→    Creates a dedicated engine from OMOP_VOCAB_URL on first call.
    54→    Raises RuntimeError if the env var is not set — callers must
    55→    handle this rather than silently degrading.
    56→    """
    57→    global _omop_engine  # noqa: PLW0603
    58→    if _omop_engine is not None:
    59→        return _omop_engine
    60→
    61→    omop_url = os.getenv("OMOP_VOCAB_URL")
    62→    if not omop_url:
    63→        raise RuntimeError(
    64→            "OMOP_VOCAB_URL environment variable is not set. "
    65→            "Set it to the OMOP vocabulary Postgres connection string "
    66→            "(e.g. postgresql://postgres:postgres@localhost:5433/omop_vocab) "
    67→            "or start the omop-vocab container: "
    68→            "docker compose -f infra/docker-compose.yml --profile omop up -d"
    69→        )
    70→
    71→    _omop_engine = create_engine(omop_url, pool_pre_ping=True)
    72→    logger.info("OMOP vocabulary engine created: %s", omop_url.split("@")[-1])
    73→    return _omop_engine
    74→
    75→
    76→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    77→# Entity type -> OMOP domain mapping
    78→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    79→
    80→ENTITY_TYPE_TO_OMOP_DOMAIN: dict[str, str] = {
    81→    "Condition": "Condition",
    82→    "Medication": "Drug",
    83→    "Lab_Value": "Measurement",
    84→    "Procedure": "Procedure",
    85→    "Demographic": "Observation",
    86→}
    87→"""Map pipeline entity types to OMOP CDM domain_id values (legacy single-domain).
    88→
    89→Demographic is a catch-all routed to Observation. Entity types not present
    90→here will fall back to Observation as well.
    91→"""
    92→
    93→ENTITY_TYPE_TO_OMOP_DOMAINS: dict[str, list[str]] = {
    94→    "Condition": ["Condition", "Observation"],
    95→    "Medication": ["Drug"],
    96→    "Lab_Value": ["Measurement", "Observation"],
    97→    "Procedure": ["Procedure", "Observation"],
    98→    "Demographic": ["Observation", "Measurement"],
    99→}
   100→"""Map pipeline entity types to OMOP CDM domain_id values (multi-domain).
   101→
   102→Primary domain is first in the list. Fallback domains follow.
   103→Candidates from the primary domain receive a small scoring bonus.
   104→"""
   105→
   106→PRIMARY_DOMAIN_BONUS: float = 0.05
   107→"""Small scoring bonus applied to candidates from the primary (first) domain."""
   108→
   109→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   110→# Result schema
   111→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   112→
   113→
   114→class OmopLookupResult(BaseModel):
   115→    """Result of an OMOP vocabulary lookup for a single entity.
   116→
   117→    Attributes:
   118→        omop_concept_id: Matched OMOP concept_id as a string, or None.
   119→        omop_concept_name: Matched concept_name from the vocabulary, or None.
   120→        omop_vocabulary_id: Vocabulary source of the match (e.g. SNOMED, RxNorm).
   121→        omop_domain_id: OMOP domain_id of the matched concept.
   122→        match_score: Fuzzy similarity score between 0.0 and 1.0.
   123→        match_method: How the match was found: "concept_name" or "synonym".
   124→    """
   125→
   126→    omop_concept_id: str | None = Field(
   127→        default=None,
   128→        description=("Matched OMOP concept_id as a string, or None if no match."),
   129→    )
   130→    omop_concept_name: str | None = Field(
   131→        default=None,
   132→        description="Matched concept_name from the OMOP vocabulary.",
   133→    )
   134→    omop_vocabulary_id: str | None = Field(
   135→        default=None,
   136→        description=("Vocabulary source of the match (e.g. 'SNOMED', 'RxNorm')."),
   137→    )
   138→    omop_domain_id: str | None = Field(
   139→        default=None,
   140→        description="OMOP domain_id of the matched concept.",
   141→    )
   142→    match_score: float = Field(
   143→        default=0.0,
   144→        ge=0.0,
   145→        le=1.0,
   146→        description="Fuzzy similarity score between 0.0 and 1.0.",
   147→    )
   148→    match_method: str = Field(
   149→        default="",
   150→        description='How the match was found: "concept_name" or "synonym".',
   151→    )
   152→
   153→
   154→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   155→# Internal helpers
   156→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   157→
   158→
   159→def _score_candidates(
   160→    entity_text: str, candidates: list[dict[str, Any]]
   161→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   162→    """Score and sort candidates by fuzzy string similarity to *entity_text*.
   163→
   164→    Uses ``difflib.SequenceMatcher`` ratio as the base score, with a bonus
   165→    for exact case-insensitive substring containment.
   166→
   167→    Args:
   168→        entity_text: The original entity text to match against.
   169→        candidates: List of candidate dicts, each with at least a
   170→            ``"match_text"`` key containing the string to compare.
   171→
   172→    Returns:
   173→        The same candidate dicts augmented with a ``"score"`` key, sorted
   174→        in descending order of score.
   175→    """
   176→    entity_lower = entity_text.lower().strip()
   177→
   178→    for candidate in candidates:
   179→        match_text = candidate.get("match_text", "").lower().strip()
   180→
   181→        # Base score: SequenceMatcher ratio
   182→        base_score = SequenceMatcher(None, entity_lower, match_text).ratio()
   183→
   184→        # Bonus for exact substring containment (either direction)
   185→        bonus = 0.0
   186→        if entity_lower in match_text or match_text in entity_lower:
   187→            bonus = 0.15
   188→
   189→        # Bonus for exact match
   190→        if entity_lower == match_text:
   191→            bonus = 0.25
   192→
   193→        candidate["score"] = min(base_score + bonus, 1.0)
   194→
   195→    # Sort by score desc, then by length similarity (prefer match_text
   196→    # closest in length to entity_text) as tiebreaker.
   197→    entity_len = len(entity_lower)
   198→
   199→    def _sort_key(c: dict[str, Any]) -> tuple[float, int]:
   200→        match_len = len(c.get("match_text", "").strip())
   201→        return (c["score"], -abs(match_len - entity_len))
   202→
   203→    candidates.sort(key=_sort_key, reverse=True)
   204→    return candidates
   205→
   206→
   207→def _get_domain_filter(entity_type: str) -> str:
   208→    """Resolve entity type to an OMOP domain_id filter value.
   209→
   210→    Falls back to ``"Observation"`` for unknown entity types.
   211→
   212→    Args:
   213→        entity_type: Pipeline entity type (e.g. "Condition", "Medication").
   214→
   215→    Returns:
   216→        OMOP domain_id string.
   217→    """
   218→    domain = ENTITY_TYPE_TO_OMOP_DOMAIN.get(entity_type, "Observation")
   219→    if entity_type not in ENTITY_TYPE_TO_OMOP_DOMAIN:
   220→        logger.info(
   221→            "Entity type '%s' not in OMOP domain map — defaulting to '%s'",
   222→            entity_type,
   223→            domain,
   224→        )
   225→    return domain
   226→
   227→
   228→def _get_domain_filters(entity_type: str) -> list[str]:
   229→    """Resolve entity type to a list of OMOP domain_id filter values.
   230→
   231→    Returns primary + fallback domains. The first domain in the list
   232→    is the primary domain (candidates from it receive a scoring bonus).
   233→    Falls back to ``["Observation"]`` for unknown entity types.
   234→
   235→    Args:
   236→        entity_type: Pipeline entity type (e.g. "Condition", "Medication").
   237→
   238→    Returns:
   239→        List of OMOP domain_id strings, primary first.
   240→    """
   241→    domains = ENTITY_TYPE_TO_OMOP_DOMAINS.get(entity_type)
   242→    if domains is not None:
   243→        return domains
   244→    logger.info(
   245→        "Entity type '%s' not in OMOP multi-domain map — defaulting to ['Observation']",
   246→        entity_type,
   247→    )
   248→    return ["Observation"]
   249→
   250→
   251→def _query_concept_table(
   252→    engine: Any, entity_text: str, domain_ids: list[str]
   253→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   254→    """Query concept table for standard concepts matching *entity_text*.
   255→
   256→    Args:
   257→        engine: SQLAlchemy engine for the OMOP vocabulary database.
   258→        entity_text: Text to search for (used in ILIKE pattern).
   259→        domain_ids: List of OMOP domain_id values to filter on.
   260→
   261→    Returns:
   262→        List of candidate dicts with keys: concept_id, concept_name,
   263→        domain_id, vocabulary_id, match_text, match_method.
   264→    """
   265→    # Build parameterized IN clause for domain_ids
   266→    domain_params = {f"d{i}": d for i, d in enumerate(domain_ids)}
   267→    domain_placeholders = ", ".join(f":d{i}" for i in range(len(domain_ids)))
   268→
   269→    sql = text(
   270→        "SELECT concept_id, concept_name, domain_id, vocabulary_id "
   271→        "FROM concept "
   272→        "WHERE standard_concept = 'S' "
   273→        f"  AND domain_id IN ({domain_placeholders}) "
   274→        "  AND concept_name ILIKE :pattern "
   275→        "ORDER BY length(concept_name) ASC "
   276→        "LIMIT :max_candidates"
   277→    )
   278→    pattern = f"%{entity_text}%"
   279→    params = {
   280→        **domain_params,
   281→        "pattern": pattern,
   282→        "max_candidates": OMOP_MAX_CANDIDATES,
   283→    }
   284→    with engine.connect() as conn:
   285→        rows = conn.execute(sql, params).fetchall()
   286→
   287→    candidates: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   288→    for row in rows:
   289→        candidates.append(
   290→            {
   291→                "concept_id": str(row[0]),
   292→                "concept_name": row[1],
   293→                "domain_id": row[2],
   294→                "vocabulary_id": row[3],
   295→                "match_text": row[1],
   296→                "match_method": "concept_name",
   297→            }
   298→        )
   299→    return candidates
   300→
   301→
   302→def _query_synonym_table(
   303→    engine: Any,
   304→    entity_text: str,
   305→    domain_ids: list[str],
   306→    *,
   307→    skip_domain_filter: bool = False,
   308→) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
   309→    """Query concept_synonym joined with concept for synonym matches.
   310→
   311→    Args:
   312→        engine: SQLAlchemy engine for the OMOP vocabulary database.
   313→        entity_text: Text to search for (used in ILIKE pattern).
   314→        domain_ids: List of OMOP domain_id values to filter on.
   315→        skip_domain_filter: If True, search across ALL domains (used for
   316→            acronym lookups where the domain may be ambiguous).
   317→
   318→    Returns:
   319→        List of candidate dicts with keys: concept_id, concept_name,
   320→        domain_id, vocabulary_id, match_text, match_method.
   321→    """
   322→    pattern = f"%{entity_text}%"
   323→
   324→    if skip_domain_filter:
   325→        sql = text(
   326→            "SELECT c.concept_id, c.concept_name, c.domain_id, "
   327→            "       c.vocabulary_id, s.concept_synonym_name "
   328→            "FROM concept_synonym s "
   329→            "JOIN concept c ON s.concept_id = c.concept_id "
   330→            "WHERE c.standard_concept = 'S' "
   331→            "  AND s.concept_synonym_name ILIKE :pattern "
   332→            "ORDER BY length(s.concept_synonym_name) ASC "
   333→            "LIMIT :max_candidates"
   334→        )
   335→        params: dict[str, Any] = {
   336→            "pattern": pattern,
   337→            "max_candidates": OMOP_MAX_CANDIDATES,
   338→        }
   339→    else:
   340→        domain_params = {f"d{i}": d for i, d in enumerate(domain_ids)}
   341→        domain_placeholders = ", ".join(f":d{i}" for i in range(len(domain_ids)))
   342→        sql = text(
   343→            "SELECT c.concept_id, c.concept_name, c.domain_id, "
   344→            "       c.vocabulary_id, s.concept_synonym_name "
   345→            "FROM concept_synonym s "
   346→            "JOIN concept c ON s.concept_id = c.concept_id "
   347→            "WHERE c.standard_concept = 'S' "
   348→            f"  AND c.domain_id IN ({domain_placeholders}) "
   349→            "  AND s.concept_synonym_name ILIKE :pattern "
   350→            "ORDER BY length(s.concept_synonym_name) ASC "
   351→            "LIMIT :max_candidates"
   352→        )
   353→        params = {
   354→            **domain_params,
   355→            "pattern": pattern,
   356→            "max_candidates": OMOP_MAX_CANDIDATES,
   357→        }
   358→
   359→    with engine.connect() as conn:
   360→        rows = conn.execute(sql, params).fetchall()
   361→
   362→    candidates: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
   363→    for row in rows:
   364→        candidates.append(
   365→            {
   366→                "concept_id": str(row[0]),
   367→                "concept_name": row[1],
   368→                "domain_id": row[2],
   369→                "vocabulary_id": row[3],
   370→                "match_text": row[4],  # synonym name used for scoring
   371→                "match_method": "synonym",
   372→            }
   373→        )
   374→    return candidates
   375→
   376→
   377→def _sync_lookup(
   378→    entity_text: str,
   379→    domain_ids: list[str],
   380→    *,
   381→    is_acronym: bool = False,
   382→) -> OmopLookupResult:
   383→    """Synchronous OMOP lookup — runs inside run_in_executor.
   384→
   385→    Queries both concept and concept_synonym tables, deduplicates
   386→    candidates by concept_id (preferring concept_name match), scores them,
   387→    and returns the best match above the threshold.
   388→
   389→    Args:
   390→        entity_text: The entity text to look up.
   391→        domain_ids: List of OMOP domain_id values to filter on.
   392→            The first domain is considered "primary" and gets a scoring bonus.
   393→        is_acronym: If True, skip domain filter for synonym search
   394→            (acronyms may appear in any OMOP domain).
   395→
   396→    Returns:
   397→        OmopLookupResult with best match, or empty result if nothing found
   398→        or all candidates score below OMOP_MIN_MATCH_SCORE.
   399→
   400→    Raises:
   401→        RuntimeError: If OMOP_VOCAB_URL is not configured.
   402→        Exception: Database errors are propagated, not swallowed.
   403→    """
   404→    engine = _get_omop_engine()
   405→
   406→    concept_candidates = _query_concept_table(engine, entity_text, domain_ids)
   407→    synonym_candidates = _query_synonym_table(
   408→        engine,
   409→        entity_text,
   410→        domain_ids,
   411→        skip_domain_filter=is_acronym,
   412→    )
   413→
   414→    # Deduplicate by concept_id — prefer concept_name match over synonym
   415→    seen: dict[str, dict[str, Any]] = {}
   416→    for candidate in concept_candidates:
   417→        seen[candidate["concept_id"]] = candidate
   418→    for candidate in synonym_candidates:
   419→        if candidate["concept_id"] not in seen:
   420→            seen[candidate["concept_id"]] = candidate
   421→
   422→    all_candidates = list(seen.values())
   423→
   424→    if not all_candidates:
   425→        logger.debug(
   426→            "No OMOP candidates found for '%s' in domains %s",
   427→            entity_text[:50],
   428→            domain_ids,
   429→        )
   430→        return OmopLookupResult()
   431→
   432→    # Score and sort
   433→    scored = _score_candidates(entity_text, all_candidates)
   434→
   435→    # Apply primary domain bonus
   436→    primary_domain = domain_ids[0] if domain_ids else None
   437→    if primary_domain:
   438→        for candidate in scored:
   439→            if candidate.get("domain_id") == primary_domain:
   440→                candidate["score"] = min(candidate["score"] + PRIMARY_DOMAIN_BONUS, 1.0)
   441→        # Re-sort after bonus
   442→        scored.sort(key=lambda c: c["score"], reverse=True)
   443→
   444→    best = scored[0]
   445→
   446→    if best["score"] < OMOP_MIN_MATCH_SCORE:
   447→        logger.debug(
   448→            "Best OMOP candidate for '%s' scored %.3f "
   449→            "(below threshold %.2f) — skipping",
   450→            entity_text[:50],
   451→            best["score"],
   452→            OMOP_MIN_MATCH_SCORE,
   453→        )
   454→        return OmopLookupResult()
   455→
   456→    logger.info(
   457→        "OMOP match for '%s': concept_id=%s, name='%s', score=%.3f, method=%s",
   458→        entity_text[:50],
   459→        best["concept_id"],
   460→        best["concept_name"][:60],
   461→        best["score"],
   462→        best["match_method"],
   463→    )
   464→
   465→    return OmopLookupResult(
   466→        omop_concept_id=best["concept_id"],
   467→        omop_concept_name=best["concept_name"],
   468→        omop_vocabulary_id=best["vocabulary_id"],
   469→        omop_domain_id=best["domain_id"],
   470→        match_score=best["score"],
   471→        match_method=best["match_method"],
   472→    )
   473→
   474→
   475→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   476→# Public async API
   477→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   478→
   479→
   480→async def lookup_omop_concept(
   481→    entity_text: str,
   482→    entity_type: str,
   483→    *,
   484→    is_acronym: bool = False,
   485→) -> OmopLookupResult:
   486→    """Look up the best OMOP standard concept for a medical entity.
   487→
   488→    Maps the entity type to OMOP domain filters (primary + fallback),
   489→    queries the concept and concept_synonym tables in the OMOP vocabulary
   490→    database, scores candidates with fuzzy string matching, and returns
   491→    the best result above the minimum similarity threshold.
   492→
   493→    The synchronous database I/O is executed in a thread-pool executor to
   494→    avoid blocking the async event loop.
   495→
   496→    Requires OMOP_VOCAB_URL to be set. Raises RuntimeError if not configured.
   497→
   498→    Args:
   499→        entity_text: The medical entity text to look up (e.g. "metformin",
   500→            "type 2 diabetes mellitus", "hemoglobin A1c").
   501→        entity_type: Pipeline entity type used to determine the OMOP domain
   502→            filter. One of "Condition", "Medication", "Lab_Value",
   503→            "Procedure", "Demographic". Unknown types fall back to
   504→            "Observation".
   505→        is_acronym: If True, skip domain filter for synonym search
   506→            to find acronyms across all OMOP domains.
   507→
   508→    Returns:
   509→        OmopLookupResult with the best-matching OMOP concept, or an empty
   510→        result if no match is found above the similarity threshold.
   511→
   512→    Raises:
   513→        RuntimeError: If OMOP_VOCAB_URL is not set.
   514→    """
   515→    if not entity_text or not entity_text.strip():
   516→        raise ValueError("Empty entity_text passed to lookup_omop_concept")
   517→
   518→    domain_ids = _get_domain_filters(entity_type)
   519→
   520→    logger.debug(
   521→        "OMOP lookup: entity='%s', type='%s', domains=%s, acronym=%s",
   522→        entity_text[:50],
   523→        entity_type,
   524→        domain_ids,
   525→        is_acronym,
   526→    )
   527→
   528→    result = await asyncio.to_thread(
   529→        _sync_lookup, entity_text, domain_ids, is_acronym=is_acronym
   530→    )
   531→
   532→    return result
   533→
```
